# Rust port test inventory

Every test case in `engine/*.test.ts`, sorted for the Rust port (issue #162,
[ADR-0036](../../adr/0036-port-to-rust-behind-a-black-box-conformance-suite.md)). The TypeScript engine's
internal tests cannot test a Rust server, so each case gets one of three fates:

- **visible**: what it checks can be seen from outside a server process. It becomes a conformance case in
  `conformance/`, written in TypeScript against a real server process, and passes against Bun before any
  Rust exists.
- **hidden**: what it checks lives inside the process. It becomes a Rust unit test, which the port ticket for
  its area must include.
- **dropped**: it tests TypeScript structure only. It needs nothing.

Written 2026-10-03 for the RUST pool, against commit `185754c`. Domain words are used as in `CONTEXT.md`;
ticket and proposal ids (conv-1-spawn-2, proposal-3 and so on) are that pool's, as its Spawn ledger listed them
that day.

The sort covers 1,295 test cases: 1,195 visible (62 of them need a seam
the server lacks today), 68 hidden and 32 dropped.

## Contract areas

Seventeen areas, chosen so that each one is a coherent thing to conform to and maps onto one stretch of the
port. A conformance run prints its pass share per area, and each port ticket names the areas it must pass.

| Area | Name | What belongs here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `formats` | On-disk formats | The bytes of the files the engine reads and writes, as such: the state line and Ticket and Conversation markdown, events JSONL, attempt log and Stream file names and their rotation, `console.json` keys and layout, `console.db` checkpoints, `runs/queued-answers.json`, `spawn-ledger.md` and the spawn files, fleet registry and Machine defaults files: parse, render, round trip, tolerance of variants. |
| `protocol` | Socket protocol | The `/api/ws` WebSocket (ADR-0032): opening, hello, the snapshot and its deltas, cards, request and reply envelopes, push coalescing, frames that cannot be sent, parity with the HTTP routes. |
| `http` | HTTP read surface | Routes no feature area owns: `GET /api/state`, the served page and its embedded snapshot, the Ticket body, log, events and activity endpoints, the pool log, pool meta. |
| `server` | Server lifecycle | The server process itself: startup (pool lock, port choice, fleet registration, the boot line), Stop from the Console, shutdown on a signal and the farewell, the Restart hand-off, dead drives reporting themselves, runtime refusals. |
| `cli` | Command lines | The boot, fleet and steward commands run as processes: pool resolution, detection, prefill, the interview, flags, what they write (`console.json`, `AGENT.md`, Setups) and what they launch. |
| `restart` | Restart and takeover | What a server starting on an existing pool does with what it finds, and what a stopping server leaves for the next: in-progress Tickets, orphan attempts (headless stopped at shutdown or killed at boot, terminal-backed re-adopted), live Conversations and Held panes, Queued answers, Pending and Held spawns, the remembered Pool workspace, checkpoints, durability after a crash. |
| `scheduling` | Scheduling and status | The drive loop: pool loading, the ready set and blocked-by, super-steps and the Super-step boundary, channels and checkpoints per super-step, the Outcome contract (an Attempt's result joining state when it exits) and the engine's own status writes, lifecycle events, adding blockers, final Review, deadlock. |
| `interrupts` | Interrupts and answers | The Interrupt mechanism: raising and listing Interrupts, answers through `/api/resume` (resume with a note, approve, Close), Keep talking and Continued attempts, Queued answers and the accept/process split for answers, persistence and Config interrupts. An Interrupt whose rule belongs to a feature (merge conflict, Selection, Review, deadlock) goes to that feature when the case is about the feature's rule. |
| `merges` | Worktrees and merges | Pool worktrees and branches, merging done work into its target, the Merge hold and Merge queue, the resolver Attempt, Ticket file reconcile at merge, untracked pool files that block a merge, merge queue enrichment. |
| `spawns` | Spawns | Spawn proposals in Outcomes (schema, ids, blocks, requested Assignment), adoption at the boundary, namespace reservation, Pending and Held spawns, Spawn caps, the Adopt, Hold and Discard routes, what the Spawn ledger lists and when it changes. |
| `attempts` | Attempts and harness launch | Launch, run and end of an Attempt, headless or terminal-backed: harness descriptors, argv, env, model and effort, the ticket prompt and its outcome and spawn instructions, the wrapper and readiness and paste echo checks, headless fallbacks when herdr refuses, the Stream file and the derived attempt log, Attempt endings (exit codes, herdr pane ends, liveness) and exit facts, Botched launches, Folder trust and Blocking dialogs, live attempts. |
| `herdr` | herdr panes | The pool's herdr panes outside an Attempt's launch and ending: the RPC client, the Pool workspace, tab labels and tab closing, pane survey and liveness, Peek and pane reads, focus, Held panes, Finished terminals, the terminal routes and the liveAttempt pane on the snapshot. |
| `conversations` | Conversations | Conversation storage, start, launch and ending, Turn state, Notices to a parent and their texts, a Conversation's spawn adoption, the conversation routes, Seeded Pools. |
| `steward` | Steward | The Steward role: what its `console.json` entry does (Steward budget, may Close), what it is told (Notices, stalled Merge queue head), its answers and their limits, Steward notes, its routes, its teaching text. The steward command line is `cli`. |
| `enlist` | Enlist | Enlisting a live herdr pane as a Ticket or a Conversation: the enlist routes, claiming and teaching the pane, the enlisted Ticket's lifecycle, never closing or deleting what the operator owns. |
| `config` | Assignments and settings | Assignment resolution (layers, effort, the verify key, the resolver's and the Steward's own Assignment), Reassign, Pool settings and Machine defaults (read, save, validation, the Steward entry's save and reload included), Config reload and its all-or-nothing rule, the settings routes, the Pool title. |
| `verify` | Verify and Jev | `verify: N` fan-out, grader Tickets, Jev grading (Evidence, rubric, fallbacks), Selection and head-to-head, human selection, Adopt a Candidate, the grades endpoint and the Winner, Jev at boot. |

When a case fits several areas, the first rule that applies wins:

1. `cli` for anything driven through the boot, fleet or steward command lines.
2. `restart` when the case is about what happens across a server stop and start (boot reconcile,
   re-adoption, resume from disk), whatever the feature.
3. `formats` when the case is about the bytes of a file (parse, render, naming, round trip), not about when
   or why the engine writes it.
4. Otherwise the feature the asserted behaviour belongs to. A feature's routes belong to the feature:
   settings and reassign routes to `config`, spawn routes to `spawns`, enlist routes to `enlist`,
   conversation routes to `conversations`, steward routes to `steward`, terminal routes to `herdr`, the
   keep-talking route to `interrupts`, grades to `verify`.
5. `http` and `protocol` only for the generic surface.

Texts handed to agents go with the feature they serve: the ticket prompt and its outcome and spawn
instructions to `attempts`, Conversation teaching and Notice texts to `conversations`, Steward teaching to
`steward`, Continued attempt teaching to `interrupts`, head-to-head and grader prompts to `verify`.

## Suggested conformance tickets

Twenty-three conformance-writing tickets. Each is one area or part of one, except C00, which is already in
the pool: proposal-3 (*Move the HTTP and socket tests onto the conformance harness*, pending at the time of
writing) moves the six files that already test through HTTP or the socket, whatever area their cases belong to.
The other twenty-two take each area's remaining visible cases, sized at 16 to 70 cases so that one
effort-max attempt can write and pass them. Every visible case sits in exactly one ticket; the render step
refuses a split that leaves a case out or puts one in two tickets. C00 is larger than the rest because it
mostly moves tests that already work through HTTP or the socket rather than writing new ones.

Every ticket waits on the conformance harness, conv-1-spawn-2. C05 and C06 also wait on the takeover harness that
proposal-4 builds (multi-leg runs), and C22 waits on open question 1.

How these tickets are meant to run:

- **Scope is the rows.** A ticket's scope is the visible rows listed under its area below, for the files and
  describe blocks it names. Its acceptance can mirror proposal-3's: every row is a passing case against
  `--server bun`, or is listed in `conformance/NOT-PORTED.md` with the reason and the Rust unit test it
  implies. Each case is tagged with its area, so the per-area pass share the runner prints means something.
- **Seams.** A row with a seam is written as the row says once its seam exists. Until then the ticket either
  writes the slower real-time version where one exists (open question 2) or lists the row in
  `NOT-PORTED.md` naming the seam. No ticket adds a seam to the server on its own: the open questions say
  which ones need the operator.
- **Order.** All of them are M0 work: the suite passes on Bun before any port ticket starts, as ADR-0036 orders
  it. Within M0, C01 (`formats`) and C02 (`protocol`) first, since M1 ports those areas first, then the areas
  M2 ports (C11 to C15, C09), then M3's (C07, C08, C10, C16 to C21, C05, C06), then M4's (C03, C04), so the
  first port tickets have their bar soonest. C22 after open question 1 is answered.
- **Gaps.** The [behaviour no test covers yet](#visible-behaviour-no-test-covers-yet) is listed per area; each
  area's ticket can take its area's entries as extra cases, or leave them for a follow-up.

## Which port work takes the hidden cases

The ADR's milestones, matched to areas. A port ticket takes every hidden row of the areas it ports, as Rust unit
tests in its acceptance, and names those areas' conformance cases as the bar it must pass. Where an area spans
two milestones the split is by layer, as shown.

| Milestone | What it ports | Areas whose hidden rows it takes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | The cargo workspace, every on-disk format, the protocol types | `formats` (0), the encoding half of `protocol` (the snapshot diff, the envelope) |
| M2 | git, the herdr client, harness launch, pane sessions | `attempts` (12), `herdr` (2), the git half of `merges` (worktrees, branches, merge mechanics) |
| M3 | The core engine | `scheduling` (2), `interrupts` (0), the decision half of `merges` (Merge hold, Merge queue, resolver), `spawns` (1), `conversations` (4), `steward` (0), `enlist` (1), `config` (0), `verify` (20), `restart` (1) |
| M4 | The HTTP and socket server, the subcommands | `http` (5), `server` (2), `cli` (2), the server half of `protocol` (push, coalescing, frames that cannot be sent) |
| M5 | Bench gates, render-survival harness, real pools, the flip | none: its bar is the bench, the harness and the whole conformance suite |

`merges` has 8 hidden rows and `protocol` has 8; the body lists them in one place, and the
two milestones that share each area divide its rows by the layer each row tests.

## How every case was counted

A test case here is one `test(` or `it(` call. Each row of the body names its call site as `file:line`, the
line of the call, followed by its describe path and name.

- **Call sites.** A TypeScript syntax-tree pass over the 48 `engine/*.test.ts` files found 1,295 calls of
  `test` or `it` imported from `bun:test` (including `it.skipIf(...)(...)` forms), with no test declared inside
  another test. A plain grep for lines that start with such a call finds the same 1,295 lines, file and
  line for file and line.
- **Runtime cases.** `bun test engine/ --reporter=junit` ran 1,326 cases (1,318 pass, 8 skip, 0 fail).
  The 31 more than the call sites come from two places: 13 calls in `attempt-run.test.ts` (lines 868 to 1287)
  sit in a loop over claude, opencode and cursor (26 extra), and `merge-hold.test.ts:102` is one `it` over a
  6-row table (5 extra). The 8 skips are `it.skipIf` cases in that loop for a harness without clear keys.
  Every runtime case maps onto exactly one call site by file and line (three report the line after the call,
  where their name sits).
- **Rows.** The body has exactly one row per call site: the render step refuses a missing, duplicate or
  unknown `file:line`, and the per-file table below agrees.

To repeat the check from the repository root:

```sh
# 1. every call site, one per line, as file:line
grep -nE '^\s*(test|it)(\.[a-zA-Z]+(\([^)]*\))?)?\s*\(' engine/*.test.ts | cut -d: -f1,2 | sed 's|^engine/||' | sort > /tmp/sites.txt
# 2. every row of this inventory, as file:line
grep -oE '^\| `[a-z-]+\.test\.ts:[0-9]+`' docs/research/rust-port/test-inventory.md | tr -d '|` ' | sort > /tmp/rows.txt
# 3. no difference, and both 1,295 long
diff /tmp/sites.txt /tmp/rows.txt && wc -l /tmp/sites.txt /tmp/rows.txt
```

## How the cases were sorted

Each case was judged by the behaviour it asserts, not by how the TypeScript test reaches it. Many engine tests
drive `runPool`, `createPoolServer` or a helper in-process with injected fakes; what decided a case was whether
its assertion would show up somewhere a black-box test can look.

**What a conformance case can set up.** A temp git repository with a pool directory in it (Ticket files,
`conversations/`, `console.json`, `AGENT.md`, `runs/` files, `held-spawns.json`, `console.db`); the server as
a separate process with its environment (`HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`, `HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID`, `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`, an
isolated `HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`); the executing fake herdr as its own process, with its controls
(rendered frames, dropped or hidden pastes, wrap width, held, injected and ended panes, workspaces, scripted
method failures, broken subscriptions); stub harness scripts on `PATH`; and the boot, fleet and steward command
lines run as processes.

**What it can observe.** The five channels the sort's ticket (conv-1-spawn-1) names (HTTP responses, WebSocket frames at `/api/ws`,
files in the pool directory, calls on the herdr socket, harness processes started: their argv, env, cwd and
prompt), plus three more that are just as observable from outside a server process and that the cases need:
the temp git repository (branches, commits, worktrees, merge results), files under the isolated `HOME` and
`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (the fleet registry, Machine defaults, claude's folder trust record), and the command
lines' own arguments, stdin, stdout, stderr, exit code and the files they write.

**The rule, in order.**

1. **drop** when the case checks only TypeScript structure with no behaviour a Rust server needs: module or
   import boundaries, TypeScript types, a test fake itself, a Bun or Node API quirk, an implementation detail
   of a helper the redesign will not have.
2. **visible** when a black-box case can observe the asserted behaviour. A real outside trigger is preferred
   (a read-only directory, a file deleted under the server, a missing binary, a killed process, a git
   conflict made in the temp repo, a fake herdr control). When the behaviour can be observed but provoking it
   needs a control the server or the rig lacks today, the case is still visible and its row names the
   **seam** it needs.
3. **hidden** otherwise: in-memory state, caching and memoisation, call counts on internal collaborators,
   ordering or timing invisible from outside, helpers whose output never leaves the process unchanged.

**How each row reads.** A conformance case is `<setup>; <action>; <observation>`. A Rust unit test is
`<subject>: <what it asserts>`, with the subject named as a concern of the redesigned internals (a pure
decision core, I/O at the edges, one task owning pool state) rather than a TypeScript function. A dropped
case gives its reason. A case inside the harness loop says it runs for claude, opencode and cursor.

**How the sort was made.** Twelve agents each sorted one batch of cases against one written brief (the areas,
the rule and the row formats above), reading each test body and the code under test; a validator checked
every batch for exactly its cases, known areas and well-formed rows. A thirteenth surveyed the server's outside
surfaces for the gaps listed below. Area boundaries are judgement calls at the edges: a case that fits two
areas sits in the one the precedence rules pick, and a conformance ticket may borrow a neighbour's row when
that reads better.

## Open questions

Decisions the conformance tickets cannot settle on their own, each with a recommendation. The first, third
and fourth change what the port must match, so they are the operator's (or the planning Conversation's) to
take.

1. **Scripted Jev answers (27 visible `verify` rows, most of C22).** The server pins Jev's base URL
   (`JEV_BASE_URL` in `engine/jev.ts`). ADR-0020 builds the client with every option explicit so that nothing
   below the CLI boundary reads the environment, and `jev.test.ts:74` checks that the client ignores
   `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL`. So no black-box case can script what Jev answers.
   - (a) The CLI boundary in `server.ts` reads a base URL beside `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` and passes it in
     explicitly, and the Rust server does the same; the rig then serves a fake TypeSafe endpoint. ADR-0020's
     rule still holds, since only the boundary reads it.
   - (b) Keep the pin. These rows become Rust unit tests of the Jev client against a fake HTTP server, and
     conformance keeps only the no-key paths.
   - Recommendation: (a). It keeps the bar black-box, which is ADR-0036's point, and the Rust client needs a
     base URL parameter for its own tests anyway. It is a change to the reference server, which this pool's
     rules leave to the operator.
2. **Fixed timings.** The pane liveness sweep (30 s) and exit-code grace (10 s), the snapshot coalescing window
   (50 ms), the enlist teaching wait (60 s), the pane survey (15 s) and the pane polls are constants, and the
   default port is 8787. About sixteen rows need one of them changed.
   - (a) Write those cases with real waits behind a slow tag, write the coalescing cases against the 50 ms
     default, and run the default-port case only when 8787 is free.
   - (b) Add environment knobs for the timings at the boundary of both servers.
   - Recommendation: (a). The server stays as it is; revisit if the slow cases make the suite too slow to run
     per ticket.
3. **Client code in `engine/protocol.ts`.** The UI and the lag bench import runtime functions from
   `engine/protocol.ts`, not only types: `applyDelta`, `encodeMessage`, `decodeServerMessage` and
   `readEmbeddedBoot` in `ui/src/socket.ts` and `ui/src/main.ts`, with `toPushed` and `diffSnapshot` building
   fixtures in `ui/src/*.test.ts` and `scripts/bench-lag/`. ADR-0036 has a generated file replace
   `protocol.ts` at the flip, which would take that code and its tests with it.
   - Recommendation: an M1 ticket, beside the protocol types and their generated-file check, that moves the
     client half of `protocol.ts` into `ui/` (or a shared TypeScript module outside `engine/`) with the cases
     that test it (`protocol.test.ts:199`, `:205` and `:317` are dropped here for that reason), so the
     generated file only has to carry types and constants.
4. **Prompts, teaching Turns and Notices: byte for byte, or by content?** ADR-0036 keeps byte-identical the
   files people and agents read (Ticket and Conversation markdown, the state line, `spawn-ledger.md`,
   `AGENT.md`). The texts the engine hands agents (the ticket prompt, the Conversation, Steward and Continued
   teachings, Notices) are read by agents too but are not files, and the rows here check what today's tests
   check: the facts each text must carry.
   - Recommendation: pin them byte for byte as well. A reworded prompt changes what agents do without failing
     any check, and the templates cost little to copy. The rows stay as they are; the tickets add a whole-text
     comparison.
5. **TypeScript behaviour that looks like a bug.** Three found while sorting, by reading the code rather than
   running it (inference):
   - some pool-load errors (an invalid `verify` value, a torn `held-spawns.json`) surface from
     `server.start()`, which `server.ts` calls after the lock, the bind and the fleet entry, so the process
     dies on an unhandled rejection and probably leaves `runs/server.pid` and its fleet entry behind, while
     other load errors exit cleanly before binding;
   - unknown paths (an unknown `/api/` route, a GET on a POST-only route, a missing asset) answer 500 rather
     than 404, because `serveStatic` tests the Promise that `Bun.file().exists()` returns;
   - `GET /api/ticket?id=01` serves whichever file `readdir` lists first among those whose name starts `01-`,
     so `01-spawn-1.md` can win over `01-a.md`.
   - Recommendation: conformance pins only the intended behaviour (exit non-zero naming the problem, a 404, the
     Ticket's own file), lists the TypeScript divergence in `NOT-PORTED.md`, and the operator decides whether
     to fix the TypeScript server first. The rule against changing the reference server makes that their call.
6. **A server that has not started its pool.** The command line starts the drive in the same tick it binds, so
   the "pool not started" refusals of `/api/resume`, `/api/stop`, the conversation, steward, spawn and
   close-finished routes, and the empty snapshot before the first run, cannot be reached from outside (two rows
   here carry that seam).
   - Recommendation: no flag. These guard a state the shipped binary never shows; they become Rust unit tests
     of the server's request handling, and the two rows go to `NOT-PORTED.md`. The rows stay marked visible
     with a seam, since a flag would make them so; this recommendation is not to add one.

**Decided** (2026-10-03, by the planning Conversation conv-1, within ADR-0036):

1. Jev: (a). The CLI boundary in `server.ts` reads a Jev base URL beside `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` and passes it in explicitly, and the Rust server does the same. A ticket makes this change to the reference server, and C22 waits on it.
2. Timings: (a). Real waits behind a slow tag, and no knobs. Revisit if the suite gets too slow to run for each ticket.
3. Client code in `protocol.ts`: as recommended. A ticket moves the client half out of `engine/` now, so the generated file only has to carry types and constants.
   Done (conv-1-spawn-26): every function `protocol.ts` held is now `ui/src/protocol.ts`, and `protocol.test.ts` moved with it to `ui/src/protocol.test.ts`, where each case sits two lines below the line the rows here cite.
4. Prompts, teaching Turns and Notices are pinned byte for byte. The tickets for those areas add a whole-text comparison.
5. Suspected bugs: a ticket fixes them in the TypeScript server, so conformance pins the intended behaviour and still passes on Bun.
6. A server that has not started its pool: no flag. These become Rust unit tests, and the two rows go to `NOT-PORTED.md`.

Notes for the conformance tickets (no decision needed):

- **Fake herdr controls.** About thirteen rows, and several gaps, need replies and events the executing fake
  cannot make yet: malformed `pane.list`, `agent.list` and `tab.create` answers, subscriber counts and
  hang-ups, a delay or no reply on a method, `tab_not_found` on `tab.close`, closing a workspace at a chosen
  request, a pane delisted without its process ending, operator-side tab and pane closes. The herdr tickets
  (C12 to C15) add them to the fake once it lives in `conformance/fixtures/`.
- **Other one-off seams (five rows).** A recording stand-in for the Boot hand-off on Restart (the rig can put a
  recording script where the server starts Boot), the other platform's `script(1)` wrapper form (the Mac run
  covers darwin), exactly one failed checkpoint write (the rig holds an exclusive SQLite lock on `console.db`
  at the right moment), and a diff git cannot compute (a `git` shim on the server's `PATH`).
- **The command lines.** The `cli` rows (C04) run boot, fleet and steward as processes. The conformance harness from
  conv-1-spawn-2 starts servers only, so C04 adds a command-line runner with the same `--server` switch
  (`bun run engine/boot-cli.ts` now, `agent-console boot` later). Boot takes `--yes` and `--no-open`, so it
  runs unattended; a stub `xdg-open` on `PATH` covers the rest.
- **Harness binaries.** cursor's binary is `agent`, so its stub goes on `PATH` under that name. Harness
  overrides and custom harness names exist only through the server's in-process `harnesses` option, so their
  cases are dropped and the rig replaces binaries on `PATH` instead.
- **The worktree key.** Pool worktrees and branches are keyed by a hash of the checkout's real path
  (`engine/worktrees.ts`), and no test pins it. A Bun, Rust, Bun run finds its worktrees only if both compute
  the same key, so the takeover cases (proposal-4) should check it; it is the first `merges` gap below.

## Visible behaviour no test covers yet

Visible behaviour found while sorting (each batch noted gaps beside the code its cases test) and in a separate
survey of the server's outside surfaces: its HTTP routes and their refusals, the socket's request and frame
kinds, the files it reads and writes, the herdr methods it calls, and the command lines' flags. A branch counted
as covered only when a test drives it. Each entry is a candidate conformance case in the row format above, with
the source line that shows the behaviour; duplicates found by more than one route are listed once. They are
candidates, not settled behaviour: where an entry says what the TypeScript server does today and that looks
wrong (the first `http` entry, the `/api/ticket` prefix ambiguity), the conformance ticket decides which answer
to pin.

### `formats`

- Ticket 01's done Outcome proposes one spawn titled Tidy up with surrounding spaces; the boundary adopts it; issues/01-spawn-1.md is exactly the state line with spawned-by=01, a blank line, the heading 01-spawn-1: Tidy up, the fixed provenance paragraph naming 01, a blank line and the trimmed body with one trailing newline (source: engine/engine.ts:9753).
- Issues/01-a.md holds a state line, a body and a stale Brief section, and the stub writes a checkpoint Outcome with brief pick a name; the attempt exits; every byte after line 1 is the body without the stale section, a blank line, three hyphens, a blank line, the Brief heading, a blank line, pick a name and one newline (source: engine/engine.ts:9211).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 8; run the pool; issues/01-grader-1.md and issues/01-head-to-head.md match the engine's grader and head-to-head Ticket templates byte for byte (state line, title, Bound artifacts line, closing paragraph), as ADR-0036 requires of Ticket markdown (source: engine/engine.ts:7450-7488 (writeGraderTicket) and `engine/engine.ts:8635-8671` (writeHeadToHeadTicket)).
- Pool with spawnCaps {perAttempt 0} and 01 proposing a Spawn whose body runs over 160 characters across several lines; let it settle; the held row in runs/spawn-ledger.md shows the body flattened to one line and cut to 159 characters plus an ellipsis. (source: engine/spawn-ledger.ts:33-43).
- Pool with Ticket 01 ready, a runs/01.log and no events file (a log from before events existed), and in a second pool only a leftover runs/01.stream.jsonl; let Ticket 01 run; the old log moves to runs/01.attempt-0.log and the lone Stream file to runs/01.attempt-0.stream.jsonl before the new attempt writes (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:364-391).
- Pool with Ticket 01 done whose runs/01.events.jsonl holds a valid scheduled line and a line whose attempt is the string "1"; GET /api/events?ticket=01; only the scheduled event comes back (source: engine/events.ts:419-426).
- For each of a Ticket file with no line-1 state line, one whose state line lacks id=, and one with status=blocked; start the server; it exits non-zero with stderr naming the file and, respectively, "has no line-1 state marker", "marker is missing id=", and "marker status must be one of ready, in-progress, done, checkpoint, closed, got 'blocked'" (the real text joins the statuses with a vertical bar) (source: engine/pool.ts:88-111 (parseMarkerLine)).
- Issues/01-a.md written with CRLF line endings, Ticket 01's stub writing a done Outcome; let the Attempt end; line 1 of the file now reads status=done (only the status= field changed) and every line still ends in CRLF (source: engine/pool.ts:156-165 (writeMarkerStatus)).

### `protocol`

- Ticket 01 at a checkpoint after attempt 1 and Ticket 02, blocked by 01, with no attempt yet, one socket subscribed to both with no follow; resume 01 so attempt 2 starts, then let 01 finish so 02 starts; 02's first card frame has log null, 01's card then gets a window naming attempt 2 with both attempts listed, and 02's card gets a window naming attempt 1 once 02 starts (source: engine/ws.ts:752).
- A terminal git pool with a Conversation started through conversations.start; subscribe with the Conversation's id, then append to its runs log; the card frame carries body null, events holding its spawned event and a log window for attempt 1 rather than an unknown ticket error, and the appended bytes arrive as an append (source: engine/ws.ts:681).
- Ticket 01 done with 200 KiB already in its attempt log; subscribe 01, append 100 KiB in one write, then 300 KiB; the window starts at totalSize minus 65536, the 100 KiB arrives as two appends of which the first holds 65536 bytes, and the 300 KiB (more than 256 KiB past the held offset) arrives as one fresh window instead of appends (source: engine/ws.ts:758).
- A done pool and one open socket; send the text not json, a binary frame holding a valid stop request, a request {id: 5, kind: bogus, payload: {}}, a request {id: -1, kind: stop, payload: {}}, then poolLog.read {before: 0}; the first four get no frame back and the pool is not stopped, the socket stays open, and only the poolLog.read reply arrives (source: engine/ws.ts:1114).
- Two sockets A and B on one pool; A sends settings.pool.put with a new title under request id 1, then B sends settings.get under its own id 1; both get the delta carrying the new poolTitle, only A gets the reply to its put, and B's only reply is its settings.get result (source: engine/ws.ts:1081).
- Pool with a graded Ticket and a socket whose hello says visible false; append a graded event to that Ticket's events file; the hidden socket still receives a live frame carrying grades, though it gets no activity or peeks (source: engine/ws.ts:614-626 and 640-650).

### `http`

- A running server; GET /api/no-such-route, GET /api/resume (a POST-only route) and GET /assets/missing.js; each should answer 404 with body not found, but the TypeScript server answers 500 with Bun's ENOENT page, because serveStatic tests the Promise that Bun.file().exists() returns, so the 404 at server.ts:2690 is unreachable (decide which answer the suite pins) (source: engine/server.ts:395).

- Herdr pool with Ticket 01 checkpointed and its pane held, the fake dropping the next pastes so the teaching Turn never lands; POST /api/keep-talking for 01; the answer is 202 for attempt 2, then runs/01.events.jsonl gets an exited event for attempt 2 with status checkpoint and a fresh checkpoint Interrupt's body says Keep talking could not teach the agent in pane <id>. (source: engine/engine.ts:4902, engine/engine.ts:4972, engine/continued.ts:262).
- Herdr pool on the default 15 s pane survey, Ticket 01 checkpointed with its pane held; end the pane through the fake and POST /api/keep-talking at once, before any survey; 409 with keep talking: ticket 01 lost its terminal: pane <id> is gone, and the next snapshot shows heldPane null. (source: engine/engine.ts:4682).
- Herdr pool whose fake holds panes open, Ticket 01 checkpointed with its pane held; make the TUI exit and POST /api/keep-talking before the next survey; 409 with lost its agent: the TUI in pane <id> has exited, and heldPane goes null. (source: engine/engine.ts:4685).
- Herdr pool with Ticket 01 continued by Keep talking after its TUI printed output; read GET /api/log?id=01&attempt=2 and attempt=1; attempt 2's log holds only the pane output written after Keep talking began (the spawned event's stream_offset), and attempt 1's log stays readable under its own number. (source: engine/engine.ts:4710, engine/engine.ts:4714, engine/continued.ts:107).

### `merges`

- A git pool whose checkout root is reached through a symlink, Ticket 01 ready and a stub that commits; let 01 run, stop the server and start another; the worktree is .git/pool-worktrees/K/01 on branch pool/K/01 where K is the first 8 hex characters of the sha256 of the checkout root's realpath, and the second server finds the same worktree and branch (source: engine/worktrees.ts:187).
- Git pool with 02 conflicting with 01 on shared.txt and a stub resolver harness that exits 3 without writing a resolution; run; one merge-conflict interrupt on 02 whose body ends "The resolver agent attempted: " plus the resolver's crash reason, and 02's worktree has no MERGE_HEAD left (the engine aborted the merge) (source: engine/engine.ts:6344-6352 and 6439-6440).
- Git pool with 02 conflicting with 01 and a stub resolver harness that writes a resolution file with no resolved boolean; run; the merge-conflict interrupt body ends "The resolver agent attempted: resolver produced no resolution" (source: engine/engine.ts:6439-6443).
- Git pool with console.json resolver "none" and 02 conflicting with 01; run; the merge-conflict interrupt body ends "The resolver agent attempted: no resolver harness available (set console.json resolver= or a ~/.issue-runner default)" and no resolver launches (the resolver-none cases assert only the kind) (source: engine/engine.ts:6314-6324 and 11718-11729).
- Git pool with a stub resolver harness that resolves 02's conflict; before approving, commit another change to shared.txt on main by hand, then approve through POST /api/resume; a merge-conflict interrupt replaces the approval saying "the resolver's resolution did not merge cleanly on approval" and the pool log reads "merge after resolver approval for 02 still conflicts" (source: engine/engine.ts:6481-6497).
- Git pool where both copies of issues/01-t.md change the same lines (as in engine.test.ts:10874); run to done; the pool log also reads "01: the pool's ticket file and the branch's copy changed the same lines; conflict markers left in issues/01-t.md" (source: engine/engine.ts:5992-5998).
- Git pool with 01 editing different regions of its worktree copy and the pool copy, its stub held open; delete runs/01.seed.md before releasing it; issues/01-t.md still holds both edits with no conflict markers, the reconcile taking the merge base's committed copy as its base (source: engine/engine.ts:6054-6082).
- Tickets 01 and 02 ready, 01's stub committing findings/x.md, a differing untracked findings/x.md in the pool checkout; let 01 finish done; the merge-conflict Interrupt body says git refused to start the merge, that nothing conflicted and the working branch was not touched, lists the files in the way, names the pool directory, and says the merge is re-attempted on resume (source: engine/worktrees.ts:528-539 (blockedMergeExplanation)).
- Tickets 01 and 02 ready in their own worktrees, both with done stubs; let both merges land; each Ticket's pool-worktrees/<key>/<id> worktree is gone from git worktree list and its pool/<key>/<id> branch is deleted (source: engine/worktrees.ts:373-378 (removeWorktree)).

### `spawns`

- Pool with Ticket 01 whose done Outcome proposes a Spawn with kind 'epic'; let it exit; runs/01.events.jsonl has spawn-rejected {index 0, reason: proposal's kind must be "ticket" or "conversation", got 'epic'} and no spawn file lands. (source: engine/engine.ts:9615-9621).
- Pool with Ticket 01 whose done Outcome proposes one Spawn with assign 'fast' and one with assign {model 3}; let it exit; spawn-rejected events read "proposal's assign is not an object" (index 0) and "proposal's assign.model is not a string" (index 1), and neither lands. (source: engine/engine.ts:9657-9669).
- Console.json with spawnCaps {perRun: 3} and nothing else; start the server; /api/state spawnUsage reads perAttempt 5 and perRun 3, and a pool with no spawnCaps at all reads perAttempt 5 and perRun 20 (each field falls back on its own) (source: engine/spawn-caps.ts:20-28).

### `attempts`

- A terminal pool whose stub claude reaches the ready frame, with pane.send_input failing after the wrapper send lands but before the prompt paste; the attempt ends with exited {code: -4} and crash reason prompt never landed, pane.close is sent for its pane, pane.release_agent follows, no headless child starts, and a crash Interrupt is raised (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:1064).

- Terminal-backed claude Ticket whose fake shows the 'Managed settings require approval' dialog; let it launch; no key is sent at it and it crashes at once with 'TUI never became ready: the managed-settings trust dialog was on screen, which only the operator may answer' (source: engine/pane-session.ts:125 and :419).
- Terminal-backed claude Ticket whose fake moves the trust dialog highlight on down but keeps the dialog on screen after enter; let it launch; after a few polls it crashes 'TUI never became ready: the workspace trust dialog was still on screen after it was answered', with the frame under '[engine] the pane showed:' in runs/01.log (source: engine/pane-session.ts:420-430, engine/attempt-run.ts:690-699).
- Terminal-backed claude Ticket whose fake answers the trust dialog correctly but never shows the ready frame; wait out the readiness bound; the crash reads 'TUI never became ready: the workspace trust dialog was answered and the ready frame still never came' and runs/01.log carries the last frame (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:1099-1106; seam: short 60 s readiness bound).
- Terminal-backed claude pool; let Ticket 01 launch and end, once done and once crashed; the fake records pane.report_agent for its pane (agent claude, state working, message '01 · <title>') once the wrapper lands, and pane.release_agent at either ending (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:667, :780, helpers at :928-958).
- Terminal-backed pool whose fake answers tab.create with a root pane terminal_id; let Ticket 01 launch; the spawned event carries that terminal_id beside pane_id and tab_id, and a headless fallback's spawned event carries none (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:999-1002; the executing fake mints term-N at engine/herdr-executing-fake.ts:350).
- Headless pool in a git checkout, stub claude, Ticket 01 on model m; let it run; the spawned event records harness claude, model m and commitSha equal to the checkout's HEAD at launch (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:971-990).
- Headless claude pool whose stub prints stream-json on stdout and a plain diagnostic line on stderr; let Ticket 01 run; the diagnostic line appears in runs/01.log and never in runs/01.stream.jsonl (source: engine/attempt-run.ts:1366 and :1419-1420).

### `herdr`

- A terminal pool launched with HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID w-launch and Tickets 01 then 02 blocked by it; once 01 is done remove w-launch from the fake and fail workspace.create; 02's tab.create is refused, workspace.get and workspace.create fail, the pool log says the Pool workspace could not be re-resolved and the attempt falls back to headless, 02's spawned event has pane_id null with a terminal_error, and 02 still finishes (source: engine/engine.ts:3111).

- Terminal-backed pool with no remembered workspace, no HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID and the fake herdr holding none; start the server; the pool log holds 'Pool workspace w1 created for this pool's tabs' (no case asserts this boot line) (source: engine/engine.ts:3034-3037).

(source: engine/engine.ts:5284-5311, engine/engine.ts:9415-9437, route engine/server.ts:2119).
- The same pool with the fake herdr scripted to refuse tab.close; POST /api/terminals/close-finished; answers {closed: 0}, runs/01.events.jsonl gains a tab-close-failed event naming the error, and the pool log says the tab could not be closed. (source: engine/engine.ts:9437-9452).
- Terminal-backed Ticket 01 running in a pane that no Turn loop watches; GET /api/terminal/peek?ticket=01; fake herdr receives pane.read {pane_id, source visible, format text, strip_ansi true} with no lines at the request, and a blank pane answers 200 with empty text rather than an error. - Pool launched with HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID=wL, and a pool whose runs/pool-workspace.json names wR without created true; change console.json's title in each; fake herdr receives no workspace.rename, the operator's workspace keeping its label. (source: engine/engine.ts:3131, engine/engine.ts:3159).
- Terminal-backed Ticket 01 live in a pane and a fake herdr that accepts pane.focus but never answers (needs that control); POST /api/terminal/focus?ticket=01; after about 10 s the answer is 502 naming 'herdr rpc timed out (pane.focus)'. (source: engine/herdr.ts:84).

### `steward`

- A started pool with a Steward on duty (enlisted with becomes steward); POST /api/steward/answer with a body that is not JSON, then with no conversation, POST /api/steward/dance, GET /api/steward/answer, and keep-talking, leave and held bodies missing their fields; 400 {reason: invalid JSON body}, 400 {reason: conversation is required}, 404 {reason: no steward route ...} twice, then 400 naming the missing fields, and nothing lands in any events file (source: engine/server.ts:2523).
- A Steward on duty, Ticket 01 at a checkpoint with its Held pane, and a Held spawn H; POST /api/steward/answer {action: resume}, then /api/steward/keep-talking {ticketId: 01, message}, then /api/steward/held {action: discard, id: H}; 202 {ok: true, message: answered 01: resume} on one checkpoint, 202 with keep talking on 01: attempt 2 continues in its pane on another with the message typed into the pane, then 200 with discarded held spawn H and H gone from heldSpawns (source: engine/server.ts:2565).
- Pool with 01 at a checkpoint whose Brief runs past 1500 characters, and a Steward; the Steward reads waiting; its Notice carries the first 1500 characters of the Brief, then '... (cut short: read the Ticket file and its log for the rest)'. (source: engine/steward.ts:370-377).
- Pool with a Steward, 01 waiting at a config Interrupt and 02 at a human selection Interrupt; the Steward reads waiting; its Notice offers 'reassign 01 field=value..., then answer 01 resume' and 'answer 02 resume <the attempt number to merge>'. (source: engine/steward.ts:395-398).
- Pool with a Steward whose pane renders busy while 01 checkpoints; the operator answers 01 through POST /api/resume, then the Steward's pane renders waiting; no Notice about 01 is typed, since a queued Steward Notice is checked against what is still pending when it is delivered. (source: engine/conversations.ts:2300-2301).

(source: engine/engine.ts:9937-9948, engine/engine.ts:10004).

### `config`

- A started pool whose console.json is hand-edited into invalid JSON; GET /api/settings, PUT /api/settings/pool {config: {title: New}}, PUT /api/reassign, GET /api/panes and the socket's panes.list; GET /api/settings answers 500 {error}, the pool PUT answers 400 and leaves the broken bytes, the Reassign answers 500, GET /api/panes falls into Bun's 500 page while panes.list replies with a clean 500 refusal, and the snapshot keeps the last good poolTitle (source: engine/server.ts:1815).
- Pool with 01 then 02 (blocked by 01); the stub for 01 rewrites console.json changing only the port; run and approve Review; no 'config reloaded' or 'config reload rejected' line is logged and no reassigned event is written, because the slice compares by value, not by file text (source: engine/engine.ts:6983-6990 and 7046-7052).
- Pool with 01 then 02 (blocked by 01); the stub for 01 changes both assign and defaults in console.json; run the pool; exactly one log line reads 'config reloaded: defaults, assign', the changed keys named in slice order (defaults, assign, resolver, spawnCaps, steward) whatever order the edit made them in (source: engine/engine.ts:6956 and 7094).
- Git pool with resolver none, 01 first, then 02 and 03 (both blocked by 01) that rewrite shared.txt differently; the stub for 01 sets resolver to claude in console.json; run until the second of 02 and 03 merges; the log holds 'config reloaded: resolver' and the conflict runs a resolver Attempt (a runs/<id>.resolver.log appears) instead of raising the manual merge-conflict Interrupt (source: engine/engine.ts:7039-7046 (resolver in the reloaded slice) and 6220-6250 (resolveResolver reads the live config at merge time)).
- Git pool with no resolver key, an isolated HOME whose ~/.issue-runner names an unknown harness, 02 conflicting with 01; run; 02 gets the manual merge-conflict interrupt and the phase never turns dead, unlike an explicit unknown resolver (engine.test.ts:12005) (source: engine/engine.ts:6240-6249).
- Settled headless pool with Ticket 01 at a checkpoint on model m; PUT /api/reassign 01 model opus, then POST /api/resume for 01; the relaunched stub argv carries --model opus and runs/01.events.jsonl gains a reassigned event from m to opus before the new spawned event, the Reassign itself having asked for no reload. (source: engine/engine.ts:7116, engine/server.ts:1860).
- Console.json with spawnCaps {perAttempt: -1} (or 1.5); start the server; it exits non-zero with 'pool config: spawnCaps.perAttempt must be a whole number, 0 or more', and the same edit made mid-run is rejected whole at the next Config reload, logged once, with the old caps still in spawnUsage (source: engine/spawn-caps.ts:37-53 (checkSpawnCaps, isSpawnCap), used by engine/engine.ts:6978 and 11916).

### `verify`

- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 5, and Ticket 02 overwriting the same cand.txt and merging first; run the pool; runs/01.events.jsonl holds a selected event for attempt 1 but no merged event, attempt 2's branch is discarded, and 01 reads status=checkpoint with a Brief naming cand.txt and pool/<key>/01.attempt-1 (source: engine/engine.ts:8486-8501 (completeSelection, a selected winner whose merge conflicts)).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 1 whose attempt commits cand.txt while an untracked cand.txt with different content sits in the checkout, grader passing; run the pool; 01 raises a checkpoint whose Brief says merging pool/<key>/01.attempt-1 was blocked and names cand.txt as in the way, nothing merges and the branch is kept (source: engine/engine.ts:8040-8053, engine/engine.ts:8109-8116 and engine/worktrees.ts:479-489 (lone attempt, merge blocked)).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 1 whose grader writes a grade with score 11, then verdict maybe, then numeric reasons; run the pool; 01-grader-1's three crash events read 'grade has no score in 0..10', 'grade verdict is not pass or flag' and 'grade has no reasons string', then the crash Interrupt lands on 01 (source: engine/engine.ts:7170-7181 (validateGrade)).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 8 whose head-to-head writes winner as the string '2'; run the pool; the selected event is attempt 2 with rule head-to-head and cand-2.txt merges (source: engine/engine.ts:8615-8620 (validateVerdict accepts a numeric string)).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 8 whose head-to-head writes a checkpoint Outcome naming winner 2; run the pool; 01-head-to-head's crash event reads 'head-to-head outcome is a checkpoint, not a pick', its card still closes done, and attempt 1 is selected by rule fallback (source: engine/engine.ts:8606-8611).
- Git pool with a verify.md holding a marker line and Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 8; run the pool; the head-to-head prompt carries verify.md verbatim and each side's grade line, such as 'First: attempt 1, graded 9/10 (pass: scored 9)' (source: engine/engine.ts:8741-8762 and engine/prompt.ts:141-151 (2974 checks paths only, with no verify.md)).
- Git pool with Ticket 01 at verify 1 whose grader flags attempt 1; after the checkpoint, rewrite verify.md and POST /api/resume 01; the second round's grader prompt carries the new verify.md text, not the old (source: engine/engine.ts:7710-7712 (verify.md read fresh at every grading round)).
- Pool outside git with Ticket 01 at verify 2 graded 9 and 5; run the pool; the pool log reads 'attempt 1 selected (the pool does not run in git; the selected work is already in the checkout)' and no merged event lands, yet 01 reads status=done (source: engine/engine.ts:8513-8516).
- Pool with Ticket 01 done and no graded events; GET /api/grades, append a well-formed graded event for attempt 1 to runs/01.events.jsonl, GET /api/grades again; the first is {} and the second carries 01's new grade, never a stale answer. (source: engine/server.ts:1229-1242).
- Ticket 05 at verify: 2 with no TYPESAFE_API_KEY and grader stubs that grade attempt 1 well above attempt 2; let Selection pick the winner; the loser's pool/<key>/05.attempt-2 branch is force-deleted although it never merged, and its worktree is removed, while its logs and Grade stay (source: engine/worktrees.ts:558-567 (discardWorktree)).

### Behaviour no black-box case can reach today

What the survey found a black-box case could observe but not provoke, with the control each would need. Open
questions 2 and 6 cover the ones that matter most.

- Pool not started: the server CLI starts the drive in the same tick it binds (server.ts:2796), so GET /api/state's null snapshot, the socket's rev 0 snapshot, and the pool not started refusals of /api/resume, /api/stop, the steward, spawn and close-finished routes and the Reassign PUT cannot be reached. Control: a flag to bind without starting the drive.
- Shutdown hard limit: a stop that hangs past 15 s exits 1 and skips the Restart hand-off to Boot (server.ts:2865). Control: a way to hang the run's shutdown, or a fake clock.
- Lock and port exhaustion: could not claim the pool lock after five attempts needs a writer that keeps runs/server.pid empty between retries, and no free port found needs every port above the start taken. Control: a lock-file racer hook and a port allocator seam.
- Fixed timings in the CLI: the 50 ms coalescing window, the 20 s heartbeat, the 15 s pane survey, 60 s readiness and teaching waits, 2 s enlisted, Continued and Conversation polls, the fleet lock's 10 s wait, Boot's 10 s boot-line and /api/state waits and its 15 s relaunch wait. Control: CLI flags or env matching createPoolServer's options, or a fake clock.
- The served page: no flag names the UI build, so a case cannot supply, rebuild, strip or remove index.html, or point the static route at known assets. Control: a dist-dir flag or env (the Rust binary embeds the UI, so decide the contract first).
- herdr replies the executing fake cannot make: a hung reply (the 10 s herdrRpc watchdog), an unparseable line or a reply closed without a newline, result-shape variants (tab.create without a tab id or root_pane, workspace.create or workspace.get without an id, pane.list without a panes array, agent.list record fallbacks), and tab_not_found on tab.close. Control: per-method raw-reply and result overrides on the fake.
- Operator-side herdr events: closing a tab or pane by hand, a silently reaped pane, subscribers dropped mid-wait, a live pane changing tab, workspace or cwd, an agent binding forgotten across a restart, and a failure on the Nth call of one burst. Control: closeTab, closePane, reapPane, dropSubscribers, updatePane, forgetAgent and failOnCall(method, n) on the executing fake.
- A crash at a chosen point: between a tmp write and its rename (console.json, held-spawns.json, queued-answers.json, steward-notes.json, pool-workspace.json, pools.json, defaults.json), mid-append of an events file, after an Outcome lands but before its exited event, between log rotation and the new log, between an orderly stop and the lock release. Control: a kill-at-point hook.
- One write failing while others succeed: the events append, the prompt file, grader diff and trim files, ENOSPC on a log mid-run, exactly N console.db writes. Control: per-path fault injection (chmod gives only EACCES and fails a whole directory, and root defeats it).
- Git failures: a whole-file merge-file conflict, merge-base or diff failing (the grader no diff texts), worktree add failing. Control: a git shim on the server's PATH that fails one subcommand.
- Socket transport: a backpressure close (a client that stops reading), the 120 s idle reap of a client that ignores pings, a socket opening inside the stop's 50 ms drain, and a snapshot or reply that cannot be encoded. Control: a slow raw client, an idle-timeout control, and a hook that plants an unencodable value (inference: likely moot in Rust).
- fs.watch failing, to reach the 2 s stat backstop for cards. Control: a way to blind the watcher.
- readdir order, to pin the /api/ticket prefix ambiguity deterministically. Control: a filesystem or hook that orders entries.
- Boot's outside effects: the browser opener (xdg-open or open), herdr detection and the terminal default, and the port 8787 probe. Control: stub xdg-open and herdr binaries on PATH, and a seam for the probe port.

## The cases, by area

<a id="formats"></a>

### `formats`: On-disk formats

The bytes of the files the engine reads and writes, as such: the state line and Ticket and Conversation markdown, events JSONL, attempt log and Stream file names and their rotation, `console.json` keys and layout, `console.db` checkpoints, `runs/queued-answers.json`, `spawn-ledger.md` and the spawn files, fleet registry and Machine defaults files: parse, render, round trip, tolerance of variants.

44 visible, 0 hidden, 3 dropped.

#### Visible: conformance cases

| Test | Conformance case |
| --- | --- |

| `attempt-run.test.ts:374` headless attempts › names a verify candidate's files by attempt and rotates nothing | Pool with Ticket 01 assigned verify: 2 and a leftover runs/01.log; let the fan-out run; each Candidate writes runs/01.attempt-N.log and runs/01.attempt-N.outcome.json under its own attempt number, and runs/01.log keeps its bytes. |

#### Dropped

| Test | Why it is dropped |
| --- | --- |
| `events.test.ts:113` ticket events read again › hands every caller its own list | drop: checks that a caller mutating the returned event list cannot change what the next caller reads, a JavaScript aliasing guard on a shared parse that Rust ownership makes moot. |
| `events.test.ts:175` attempt stream naming › parses back the four shapes it names | drop: exercises a Stream file name parser that no engine path calls; the server never reads Stream file names back, and the names it writes are covered by the stream naming cases. |
| `events.test.ts:193` attempt stream naming › rejects names outside the contract | drop: exercises the rejections of a Stream file name parser that no engine path calls; nothing outside can observe it, and the names the engine writes are covered by the stream naming cases. |

<a id="protocol"></a>

### `protocol`: Socket protocol

The `/api/ws` WebSocket (ADR-0032): opening, hello, the snapshot and its deltas, cards, request and reply envelopes, push coalescing, frames that cannot be sent, parity with the HTTP routes.

40 visible, 8 hidden, 4 dropped.

#### Visible: conformance cases

| Test | Conformance case |
| --- | --- |
| `protocol.test.ts:92` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › say nothing changed when nothing did | Quiescent pool and an open socket at revision R; send a reassign setting Ticket 02's model to the model its assign entry already names, which re-reads the snapshot; no delta frame arrives and the reply's rev is still R. |
| `protocol.test.ts:97` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › carry a changed ticket alone, keeping its neighbours by reference | Quiescent pool with Tickets 01, 02 and 03 and an open socket; reassign Ticket 01 to model x-model; the delta lists only 01 under tickets.upsert, carries no tickets.order, and does not resend 02 or 03. |
| `protocol.test.ts:114` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › add and remove tickets, with the order they now stand in | Pool with Tickets 01, 02 and 03 (02 and 03 blocked by 01) and an open socket; delete 02's Ticket file, write a Ticket 04, then trigger a re-read (a reassign of 03 to its current model); the delta has 04 under tickets.upsert, [02] under tickets.remove and tickets.order [01, 03, 04]. |
| `protocol.test.ts:122` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › reorder tickets by id without resending them | Pool with Tickets 01, 02 and 03 and an open socket; rename 01's Ticket file to sort last (its state line id unchanged), then trigger a re-read (a reassign of 02 to its current model); the delta's tickets field is only {order: [02, 03, 01]}, nothing resent. |
| `protocol.test.ts:130` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › key Conversations the same way | Pool with the executing fake herdr, Conversation c1 live and an open socket; start Conversation c2 through POST /api/conversations; the delta's conversations.upsert holds c2 (not an unchanged c1), conversations.order lists c1 then c2, and no tickets field is sent. |
| `protocol.test.ts:141` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › append new pool log lines rather than resend the log | Ticket 01 at a checkpoint and an open socket; resume 01 so the engine logs new pool log lines; the delta's log field is {append: only the new lines, total: the whole log's length}, never the whole log again. |
| `protocol.test.ts:149` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › append across the window's edge, and say how long the whole log is | Pool whose log already holds more than 500 lines (many quick Tickets, or a restored checkpoint) and an open socket; two more lines are logged; the delta's log is {append: exactly those two lines, total: the full log length}, even though the socket's window was cut to 500. |
| `protocol.test.ts:184` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › replace a changed state field whole and leave untouched lists by reference | Ticket 01 running under a stub that waits for a release file and then writes a checkpoint Outcome, and an open socket; release it; the delta's state field carries interrupts whole and leaves out every state field that did not change, such as config and mergeQueue. |
| `protocol.test.ts:211` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › chain over many versions to what the server holds | Two Tickets run to the Review gate with one socket open from the start; apply every delta in order to that socket's first snapshot frame; the result equals the snapshot frame a socket opened at the end receives, at the same revision. |
| `protocol.test.ts:229` trimPoolLog › keeps a short log whole | Pool with a log under 500 lines and an open socket; compare the snapshot frame with GET /api/state; the frame's state.log is the whole log and logTotal is its length. |
| `protocol.test.ts:233` trimPoolLog › keeps the last window of a long one and counts it all | Pool whose log holds N lines, N over 500; open a socket; the snapshot frame's state.log holds exactly the last 500 lines in order and logTotal is N. |
| `protocol.test.ts:247` trimPoolLog › trims the pushed snapshot's log and leaves the full one alone | Pool whose log holds just over 500 lines; open a socket and GET /api/state; the snapshot frame carries 500 lines with logTotal the full count, while GET /api/state still returns every line. |
| `protocol.test.ts:258` the envelope › round-trips a request and its reply | Open socket on a pool where Ticket 01 has no pane; send {type: request, id: 3, kind: terminal.focus, payload: {ticketId: 01}}; the answer is {type: reply, id: 3, kind: terminal.focus, rev, ok: false, refusal: {reason, status: 404}}. |
| `protocol.test.ts:276` the envelope › refuses what is not this protocol's | Open socket; send six frames that are not this protocol's: non-JSON text, an array, type nope, a request of kind 'rm -rf', a request with no id, a subscribe with no card; none is answered, the socket stays open and the next valid request is answered. |
| `server.test.ts:752` pool server › sends the latest snapshot to a socket on connect | Pool run to phase done (REVIEW approved); open a socket on /api/ws; the first frame is hello and the first snapshot frame carries the latest snapshot, phase done. |
| `server.test.ts:768` pool server › keeps the socket open through more than ten seconds of a quiet pool | Pool settled at 01's checkpoint interrupt with nothing more to push; open a socket, wait 14 s, then resume 01 through /api/resume; the socket is still open after the silence (past HTTP's 10 s idle cut) and delivers a delta frame for the resume. |
| `server.test.ts:804` pool server › sends a heartbeat frame on the socket | Settled pool with nothing to push; open a socket and wait past the heartbeat interval its hello announced (20 s by default); a frame exactly {type: heartbeat} arrives. |
| `server.test.ts:822` pool server › opens the socket with a hello publishing the heartbeat interval | Settled pool; open a socket on /api/ws; the very first frame, ahead of the snapshot, is hello with protocol 1 and heartbeatMs equal to the server's heartbeat interval (20000 by default). |
| `server.test.ts:847` snapshot push coalescing › pushes every emit as its own frame with a zero window | Pool with Ticket 01 ready and a snapshot coalescing window of 0; open a socket as soon as the server listens and let the run settle; each snapshot or delta frame's seq is one more than the last, more than two frames in all, up to /api/state's final seq. *Seam: a settable snapshot coalescing window.* |
| `server.test.ts:861` snapshot push coalescing › pushes a burst of emits as one frame carrying the latest | Pool with Ticket 01 ready and a 500 ms coalescing window; open a socket, let the run settle and read /api/state at once; /api/state already carries the final seq, while the socket gets fewer frames than the seqs it spans, in ascending order, its last frame at the final seq. *Seam: a settable snapshot coalescing window.* |
| `server.test.ts:884` snapshot push coalescing › pushes a waiting snapshot before the sockets close | Pool run to phase done with a 60 s coalescing window; open a socket, then POST /api/stop; the stop answers 202 and the waiting snapshot is pushed before the socket closes with code 1000 reason stopped, so the socket's last snapshot has phase stopped. *Seam: a settable snapshot coalescing window.* |
| `ws.test.ts:233` opening a socket › says hello, then sends the snapshot, without waiting for the client | Pool with Ticket 01 and a stub harness; open a socket and send nothing, then open a second socket once the run settles; the first frames are hello {protocol 1, epoch, heartbeatMs 20000} then a whole snapshot frame, and the later socket gets the same hello and starts at the first one's revision and snapshot. |
| `ws.test.ts:261` opening a socket › refuses a plain request at the socket's path | Running server; plain GET /api/ws with no upgrade headers; status 400 with body 'expected a WebSocket upgrade'. |
| `ws.test.ts:270` opening a socket › refuses a socket opened from another site's page, and opens one from its own page or none | Running server; GET /api/ws with Origin http://evil.example, null or another localhost port, then open sockets with the server's own Origin and with none; the first three answer 403 'cross-origin socket refused' and cannot open, the last two open, say hello and answer settings.get. |
| `ws.test.ts:294` the snapshot push › sends every socket the same delta per coalesced change, each on from the last | Pool with Ticket 01 and 02 blocked by it, two open sockets; run to the Review gate and approve; both sockets get byte-identical snapshot and delta frames, each delta's base is the previous rev and its rev one more, deltas are fewer than engine emits (seq), and the applied tickets match GET /api/state. |
| `ws.test.ts:335` the snapshot push › sends a hidden socket its deltas and heartbeats too | Pool with Ticket 01 and a socket whose hello says visible false; let the run settle and wait past the 20 second heartbeat; the hidden socket still receives every delta up to the settled snapshot, and a heartbeat frame. |
| `ws.test.ts:350` requests › replies to an action after the delta that carries its effect | Ticket 01 at a checkpoint Interrupt and an open socket; send a resume request for 01; the delta removing 01's Interrupt arrives before the reply, and the reply's rev equals that delta's revision. |
| `ws.test.ts:382` requests › replies to a settings save and a Reassign after their deltas, with none behind | Ticket 01 at a checkpoint, 02 blocked, an open socket; send settings.pool.put title Renamed, then reassign 02 to model x-model, then settings.machine.put with empty defaults; each of the first two replies follows exactly one delta carrying its change and none behind, the third follows no delta and names the revision already held. |
| `ws.test.ts:470` requests › refuses with the HTTP twin's status, for every status class | Pool with no herdr daemon and an open socket; send resume without ticketId, adopt with attempt 1.5, terminal.focus for an unknown ticket, stop before done, reassign after console.json turns unreadable, panes.list, and a request with an id but no payload; refusals carry the HTTP twin's reason and status (400, 400, 404, 409, 500, 502, 400), and an unknown frame type gets no reply. |
| `ws.test.ts:735` cards › sends a subscribed card whole in one frame, then its log's appends and its events | Finished pool and an open socket; subscribe card 01, append to its log, append an event to runs/01.events.jsonl, unsubscribe, append again; one card frame brings body, events and a log window (attempt 1, offset 0), the append arrives from its nextOffset within 1.5 seconds, the event arrives as card events, and nothing follows the unsubscribe. |
| `ws.test.ts:797` cards › answers a card the pool does not know with an error | Finished pool and an open socket; subscribe card 99, an id the pool does not know; the answer is {type: card, id: 99, error: 'unknown ticket 99'} and later file changes bring nothing for 99. |
| `ws.test.ts:805` cards › holds 32 cards a socket at most, and turns away unknown ids without reading the disk | Pool of 34 done Tickets and an open socket; send a hello naming all 34 cards, subscribe the 34th, send a hello of 2000 unknown ids, then a frame over 64 KiB; 32 cards arrive whole, the 34th is refused 'a socket holds at most 32 cards', the unknown ids get 32 'unknown ticket' errors at once, and the big frame closes the socket. |
| `ws.test.ts:862` cards › refuses a card it cannot read without holding it, and reads it afresh once it can | Pool with Tickets 01 and 02, 02's Ticket file at mode 000 (non-root run); open a socket whose hello subscribes 02 and 01; card 02 is refused with an error containing 'could not be read' while 01 arrives whole, and once the mode is restored a new socket subscribing 02 gets its body and events. |
| `ws.test.ts:899` cards › leaves each event's logTail out of a card, and keeps it in GET /api/events | Finished pool whose exited events carry payload.logTail; compare GET /api/events?ticket=01 with the events in card 01's frame; the HTTP events keep logTail and the card's are the same events with logTail removed. |
| `ws.test.ts:921` cards › never splits an escape sequence across two appends | Finished pool with card 01 subscribed; append 'red ' plus a partial escape sequence (ESC [38;5) to its log, then the rest ';196mtext', ESC [0m, ' done' and a newline; the first append carries only 'red ' (GET /api/log from that offset stops there too), the second 'text done', together 'red text done'. |

| `ws.test.ts:977` cards › points a card's appends at another attempt with log.follow, and reads any range with log.read | Ticket 01 checkpointed on attempt 1 and finished on attempt 2, card 01 subscribed; send log.follow for attempt 1, then append to both attempts' logs; the reply equals GET /api/log?ticket=01&attempt=1&offset=0 (a short file) plus attempt 1 and stream false, appends follow attempt 1 only, a follow with attempt null names 2, and log.read equals GET /api/log. |
| `ws.test.ts:1032` the live check › sends a hidden socket no live values, and the whole of them when it shows | Ticket 01 running, with one socket hello'd visible and one hidden; wait for live frames, then send visibility true on the hidden one; the visible socket gets live activity for 01 with running true, the hidden one gets no activity or peeks until it shows, then a live frame with that same activity. |
| `ws.test.ts:1068` the live check › peeks a new pane at once, and pushes its text again only when it moves | Terminal-backed pool on the executing fake herdr, Ticket 01 running in a pane, an open socket; watch live frames, then set the pane's content to 'working'; a live frame with peeks.01 {ticket, paneId} comes within 1 second of the pane appearing, the moved text comes once as 'working', and no repeat follows over the next 2 seconds. |
| `ws.test.ts:1223` socket and HTTP parity › answers every request kind as its HTTP twin does | Pool run to done and an open socket; ask each of the 22 request kinds on the socket and over its HTTP twin, in a state where asking twice answers alike (stop and restart each on a server of its own, as both end it); each reply equals the twin's body less snapshot, or its error and status, across ok, 400, 404 and 409. |

| --- | --- |
| `engine.test.ts:360` snapshot history › keeps the last snapshot only when asked to, with the seq still counting every emit | snapshot history: when bounded to one, the pool keeps only its newest snapshot while seq still numbers every emit from 0 without gaps, so a finished one-ticket run keeps a single snapshot with phase done and seq above 2. |
| `protocol.test.ts:161` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › replace a log that did not simply grow | snapshot delta: a pool log that did not simply grow since the last pushed version (its kept lines differ from the old window's tail) goes as a replace of the whole window with the new total, never spliced on. |
| `protocol.test.ts:169` diffSnapshot and applyDelta › replace a changed top-level field whole and drop one that went | snapshot delta: a changed top-level field goes whole under set, and a top-level field that is no longer present is listed under unset and removed on apply. |
| `ws.test.ts:533` a push or a reply that cannot go › refuses a result it cannot serialise, keeps the socket, and answers the next request | socket replies: a request result that cannot be encoded is answered as a refusal with status 500 and a reason starting 'could not encode the result: ', it is logged, and the socket stays open and answers the next request. |

| `ws.test.ts:655` a push or a reply that cannot go › never counts a first snapshot it cannot encode, and sends the next good one whole | snapshot push: a first snapshot that cannot be encoded is logged and never counted pushed, sockets keep the null snapshot of revision 0, and the next good version goes whole as revision 1, not as a delta. |
| `ws.test.ts:685` a push or a reply that cannot go › logs a pushed version that no longer encodes whole, drops only the socket opening on it, and serves the page bare | socket open and page: when the version the sockets hold cannot be encoded whole, only the socket opening on it is dropped with no snapshot frame, sockets already open are untouched, and the page is served as built, without the embedded boot, with cache-control no-store. |

<a id="cli"></a>

### `cli`: Command lines

The boot, fleet and steward commands run as processes: pool resolution, detection, prefill, the interview, flags, what they write (`console.json`, `AGENT.md`, Setups) and what they launch.

58 visible, 2 hidden, 1 dropped.

<a id="restart"></a>

### `restart`: Restart and takeover

What a server starting on an existing pool does with what it finds, and what a stopping server leaves for the next: in-progress Tickets, orphan attempts (headless stopped at shutdown or killed at boot, terminal-backed re-adopted), live Conversations and Held panes, Queued answers, Pending and Held spawns, the remembered Pool workspace, checkpoints, durability after a crash.

84 visible, 1 hidden, 0 dropped.

#### Visible: conformance cases

| Test | Conformance case |
| --- | --- |
| `attempt-ending.test.ts:248` waitForPaneEnding › ends immediately on a file that is already there | Terminal-backed pool stopped while Ticket 01's attempt runs in a pane the fake keeps open; while the server is down the stub exits 0 with no Outcome, leaving runs/01.exitcode; restart the server; the re-adopted attempt ends at once (crash 'no outcome written') and the fake records no events.subscribe for its pane. |
| `attempt-ending.test.ts:361` waitForAttemptEnding with a headless watch › names a shutdown stop as what it was, not as the harness's failure | Headless pool, stub claude that sleeps; send the server SIGTERM mid-attempt; runs/01.events.jsonl's crash reason reads 'harness stopped by engine shutdown (exited 143)', no crash Interrupt is raised, and issues/01.md is left in-progress for the next boot. |
| `attempt-ending.test.ts:390` waitForAttemptEnding's boot fast path › decides on the exit-code file already on disk, without a daemon | Terminal-backed pool stopped mid-attempt, the fake keeping the pane; while it is down the stub exits 7 with no Outcome, leaving runs/01.exitcode; restart the server; the re-adopted attempt is decided from the file alone: exited code 7 and crash 'harness exited 7', with no events.subscribe for its pane. |
| `attempt-ending.test.ts:405` waitForAttemptEnding's boot fast path › reads an outcome written while the engine was down as the clean ending | Terminal-backed pool stopped mid-attempt, the fake keeping the pane open; while it is down the stub writes a done Outcome and exits 0; restart the server; the re-adopted attempt ends clean: exited code 0 status done, no crash event, and Ticket 01 goes on to its merge. |

| `children.test.ts:62` ChildTracker › stopAll falls through to KILL when a child ignores TERM | Headless pool whose stub claude traps and ignores TERM; send the server SIGTERM; the stub survives the TERM and is gone once the 5 s grace has passed, its crash reading 'harness stopped by engine shutdown (exited 137)', and the server exits after it. |

<a id="scheduling"></a>

### `scheduling`: Scheduling and status

The drive loop: pool loading, the ready set and blocked-by, super-steps and the Super-step boundary, channels and checkpoints per super-step, the Outcome contract (an Attempt's result joining state when it exits) and the engine's own status writes, lifecycle events, adding blockers, final Review, deadlock.

43 visible, 2 hidden, 0 dropped.

#### Visible: conformance cases

| Test | Conformance case |
| --- | --- |
| `engine.test.ts:392` pool loading › rejects a pool with no Issue files | Pool with an empty issues/ directory and no conversations/ directory; start the server; the process exits non-zero with stderr 'pool load: no Issue files in' that names a conversations/ directory as the Seeded Pool opt-in. |
| `engine.test.ts:3427` super-steps › runs independent ready tickets as one super-step and terminates done | Pool with ready Tickets 01 and 02 (no blockers) and a stub claude writing done Outcomes; start the server and approve Review via POST /api/resume; the pool log holds 'super-step 1: 01, 02' and ends 'pool done: every ticket reached done', phase is done and both state lines read status=done. |
| `engine.test.ts:3466` super-steps › keeps a ticket waiting until every blocker is done | Pool with 01 ready and 02 blocked-by=01, stub writing done Outcomes; start the server and approve Review; the pool log holds 'super-step 1: 01' and 'super-step 2: 02', and some /api/ws snapshot shows 01 in-progress while 02 is still ready. |
| `engine.test.ts:3498` super-steps › runs the ready set against one shared starting snapshot | Pool with 01 ready and 02 and 03 both blocked-by=01, a stub that records its prompt and writes a done Outcome summarised summary-&lt;id&gt;; run the pool; the log holds 'super-step 2: 02, 03', the prompts of 02 and 03 both contain summary-01 and neither holds the other's summary, and state.outcomes has 01, 02 and 03. |
| `engine.test.ts:3613` ticket events › records a checkpoint and resume in order, numbering attempts per ticket | Pool with 01 ready and 02 blocked-by=01, a stub that checkpoints the first run of 01 and finishes every other run done; resume 01 with a note via POST /api/resume and approve Review; runs/01.events.jsonl reads scheduled, spawned, exited, checkpoint, answered as attempt 1 then scheduled, spawned, exited as attempt 2, each exited payload {code 0, status, logTail [], outcomeExists true}, and 02 runs once as attempt 1. |

<a id="interrupts"></a>

### `interrupts`: Interrupts and answers

The Interrupt mechanism: raising and listing Interrupts, answers through `/api/resume` (resume with a note, approve, Close), Keep talking and Continued attempts, Queued answers and the accept/process split for answers, persistence and Config interrupts. An Interrupt whose rule belongs to a feature (merge conflict, Selection, Review, deadlock) goes to that feature when the case is about the feature's rule.

49 visible, 0 hidden, 0 dropped.

| Test | Conformance case |
| --- | --- |

Pool worktrees and branches, merging done work into its target, the Merge hold and Merge queue, the resolver Attempt, Ticket file reconcile at merge, untracked pool files that block a merge, merge queue enrichment.

50 visible, 8 hidden, 1 dropped.
